# Session notes — 2026

Per-session decision log, newest first. Archived from the root
[SESSION_NOTES.md](../../SESSION_NOTES.md) on 2026-10-27 (SN-1) and kept as the
running 2026 file: new entries go at the top of the list, directly below the
separator; the root keeps the current state plus the
[folder index](README.md).

Entries are historical records — numbers, paths and line counts were true when
written. Where an entry conflicts with the code, the code wins.

---

## ✅ Round 7 closed — the last legacy device read is gone (2026-10-27)

Round 7 shipped CDR-2 (`46fefb6`), the follow-up CDR-1 recorded: the
`customerDevices` bootstrap slice — the last path that answered a device
request with the whole `customer_device_records` table, measured at 5,414.2 KiB
raw / 431.5 KiB gzip for 12,000 rows (52.87 MiB / 4.21 MiB at the 10× model) —
is gone. The mapping moved out of `BOOTSTRAP_TABLES` into the new
`CACHE_ONLY_TABLES` export, so `fetchBootstrapSlice` can no longer resolve the
key while `CACHE_LOADS` keeps the operational device mirror hydrated for
global search, the CPE/CDR flows and the approval/serial checks; the
controller answers the key with **410 Gone** (`sliceRetired: true`) and left
the 503 fallback list. The measurement also settled why the slice was worth
removing beyond its size: the `/bootstrap/local` branch guard covers
`/bootstrap` only, so a branch account that omitted `branchId` read all 12,000
rows across both branches, where the paged route refuses that request with
403. The close pass ran the whole chain (`tsc`; **1354 tests, 321 suites, 1352
pass, 0 fail, 2 Windows-only skips** in 340.1 s; docs-count 1354;
`no-inline-sql` 16 controllers; `no-raw-dialogs` 161 client files; build;
bundle budget **281.6 kB / 15.8 kB** of 320/17, unchanged — no client source
moved) and swept the worklist/notes links, fixing the CDR-2 entry's note
reference and the archived round README for their new depth under `done/`.
Every pushed state stayed CI-green (CDR-2 `46fefb6` run 184, mark `4e19b39` run
185, and this close pass `3ed11c0` run 186). No round is active; round
archive:
[worklist/done/2026-10-27-round-7.md](../../worklist/done/2026-10-27-round-7.md).

## ✅ CDR-2 — the last legacy device read is gone (2026-10-27)

CDR-1 left one whole-table device read alive: the `customerDevices` bootstrap
slice, which answered every row of `customer_device_records` to a cached
pre-VOL-4b bundle that no current screen can call, while its mapping also fed
the operational cache. CDR-2
([worklist item](../../worklist/done/2026-10-27-round-7/CDR-2.md), `46fefb6`) split the
mapping — the config moved to a new `CACHE_ONLY_TABLES` export, so
`fetchBootstrapSlice` can no longer resolve the key while `CACHE_LOADS` keeps
the device mirror hydrated — and the controller now answers
`key=customerDevices` with **410 Gone**, naming the replacement reads. Measured
through the real mapping before the change: 12,000 rows were 5,414.2 KiB raw /
431.5 KiB gzip (462.0 B / 36.8 B per row; 10× model 52.87 MiB / 4.21 MiB), and
a branch account that omitted `branchId` read all 12,000 rows across both
branches — the paged route refuses that with 403. Full gates before the commit:
1354 tests, 321 suites, 1352 pass, 0 fail, 2 Windows-only skips in 338.2 s,
docs-count 1354 and both convention guards, the whole chain exiting 0; CI run
184 green in 363 s. The register, overview and warranty reads are untouched.

or `client/` changed for the decision, as its acceptance required; both firing

run 175, STOCK-1 `9009f9c` run 176, STOCK-2 `9fec135` run 177, and this close

fixed a deferred defect of the SN-1 split: 62 root-relative links in this file

## ✅ Round 5 watch-list measured — none of the remaining arrays earns an item (2026-10-27)

Round 4 left three uncapped arrays on `/api/bootstrap` — `inventory_stock`,
`damage_records` and the two return registers — with a standing note to measure
before opening an item. Measured 2026-10-27 on the local demo DB (realistic
scratch rows fetched through the real `BOOTSTRAP_TABLES` mappings, inserted in a
transaction that was rolled back), with volumes modelled on the recorded B1
dataset:

| Array | Row width (raw / gzip) | Volume model | Payload per login |
|---|---|---|---|
| `stock` (inventory_stock) | 143 B / 27 B | catalog rows per branch; catalog × branches on a global (ALL) read | 2,000 products ≈ 279 KiB raw / 53 KiB gzip per branch; 2,000 × 20 ≈ 5.46 MiB raw / 1.03 MiB gzip global |
| `damageRecords` | 715 B / 55.7 B | upper bound: every recorded stock operation is a damage row (12k / 120k) | ≤ 8.18 MiB raw / 653 KiB gzip at 1×; ≤ 81.8 MiB / 6.37 MiB at 10× |
| `purchaseReturns` | 1,100 B / 137.9 B | recorded B1 model (600 / 6,000) | 645 KiB raw / 81 KiB gzip at 1×; 6.29 MiB / 808 KiB at 10× |
| `salesReturns` | 1,104 B / 138.2 B | recorded B1 model (900 / 9,000) | 970 KiB raw / 122 KiB gzip at 1×; 9.48 MiB / 1.19 MiB at 10× |

**Verdict: no Round 5 item.** The two return registers together are ≈ 203 KiB
gzip at the recorded 1× — the smallest arrays still on the bootstrap, below
every one already removed (shipments 300 kB, customers 284 kB, devices
435 kB) — and both are fiscal-year scoped, so they age out. Damage is FY-scoped
too, and even at an impossible every-stock-operation-is-damage bound it stays
below the devices array at 1×. `inventory_stock` is the only array that can
outgrow them, because it has no fiscal-year scope: a branch read is
catalog-sized (hundreds of KiB raw at 500–5,000 products), while a global read
is catalog × branches.

**Recorded trigger:** revisit `inventory_stock` when a global (ALL-scope)
login's stock array passes ≈ 1 MiB gzip (catalog × branches ≈ 2,000 × 20), or
when a stock screen needs server-side search/paging — and re-measure first,
because the catalog × branch sizes are stated assumptions, not recorded
volumes (the demo DB holds 18 products, 3 branches and 32 stock rows).

**Consumers, so the verdict is not misread.** Both return registers already
read the paged `/api/purchase-returns` / `/api/sales-returns` endpoints; the
bootstrap arrays are only the degraded fallback when that fetch fails, so
removing them would follow the VOL-2 fallback-removal precedent, not open a
payload item. `DamagedStockTracking` and the stock screens (All Available
Stock, Branch Stock, Physical Stock Audit) genuinely filter their arrays in the
browser, so moving those is a screen migration, not just a payload deletion.

**Limitations.** Row widths are measured on realistic scratch rows, not
production data; the scratch transaction was rolled back and the DB is
untouched. The returns volume model is the B1 dataset; damage has no recorded
volume, so its row shows an upper bound; the stock scenarios (500/2,000/5,000
products × 20/50 branches) are assumptions. The measurement script was
removed after the run; re-derive it from the mapping-driven approach above if
the trigger fires.

## ✅ EXCH-1 — the Device Exchange picker searches the paged device read (2026-10-27)

Round 4's close-out item. The Device Exchange tab was the last consumer of the
bounded legacy `GET /api/customer-devices` array: the host fetched it once per
mounted StockOperations host and the panel filtered it in the browser, so a
branch (or a global view) above the 2,000-newest-row cap could not reach — let
alone exchange — an older deployed unit.

The picker now reads `GET /api/customer-devices/page` through the existing
shared per-scope cache (`exchangeDeviceStore`): the header branch, the
RENTAL/ACTIVE eligibility filter and the typed term all go to the server and are
matched through `CDR_SEARCH_TEXT_SQL` / `idx_customer_devices_global_trgm`; the
page is 50 rows plus the exact matching total. The term lives in the host (so
the tab counter and the picker describe the same scope), is debounced (250 ms),
and each term is its own cache scope, released when the input moves on, so an
abandoned search is not refetched on every SSE burst. The previous page of the
same branch stays on screen while the next one loads; a branch switch, a failed
read or a failed search shows nothing rather than another (or an older) branch's
rows.

Measurement (scratch rows inserted and rolled back on the local demo DB): one
device row serializes to 549 B (15 columns, `CDR_SELECT_COLUMNS`), so the legacy
picker read carried up to ≈ 1.05 MiB per branch at its 2,000-row cap while the
paged read carries ≈ 26.8 KiB for the same row shape — and the searched page can
return a unit the cap dropped entirely (the store suite pins a term that finds a
row outside page 1 of a 4,700-row matching set).

Acceptance: a unit outside the first page is reachable by search and the store
keeps the returned `CustomerDeviceRecord`; no client screen calls
`api.getCustomerDevices` any more (a source walk fails the suite if one comes
back); the replacement refusal path (IN_STOCK unit of the same branch and
product, refusal audit) is unchanged; retry/error/branch scoping are pinned by
the cache tests.

Local gates green: `tsc`; the five focused suites (exchange/store, device-page
contract, refusal audit, refresh domains, picker load schedule) **83/83 pass**;
full `npm test`
**1344 tests (1342 pass, 0 fail, 2 Windows-only skips)** with the docs-count
gate at 1344; `no-inline-sql`; `no-raw-dialogs`; build; bundle budget
**15.7 kB entry / 281.2 kB startup** (17 kB / 320 kB). Evidence:
`tests/exchangeDevices.test.ts` and `tests/exchangeDeviceLoadSchedule.test.ts`.
Landed as `3d7afe4`; CI run
[40456200247](https://github.com/yogneoz/ISP-Inventory-Management-System/actions/runs/40456200247)
green in 373 s.

## ✅ ASSET-1 — fixed assets leave the bootstrap (2026-10-27)

Round 4's scale item. `fixed_assets` was the last table whose array size
followed the installed base and never aged out by fiscal year: the bootstrap
read every row (29 aliased columns) and App handed the same array to 19
screens. It is now served by `GET /api/fixed-assets/page` (branch/status/
category/assigned/text/date filters, 200-row cap, `all=1` bounded export),
`/overview` (status counts, formula totals, `byBranch`, `groupBy=master`, the
closing diagnostics), `/available` (bounded ACTIVE/unassigned picker list) and
`/lookup`. Depreciation moved to `shared/depreciation.ts`; the server aggregates
run that same function, so totals cannot disagree with the rendered rows.

Both registers page on the server, the dashboard/statements/closing wizard read
overview totals, and StockOperations reads the shared `/available` cache
through `useAvailableFixedAssets`. App holds no `assets` state; `fixedAssets`
is a register key bumped by `ASSETS` and `RECALC`. The local slice endpoint
still answers `key=assets` for an older bundle (honest 503 when its live read
fails).

All three asset CSV exports fetch the bounded report and alert when the server
caps it at 20,000 rows, so an export never stops silently at a page.

Measurement (local demo DB, 3 rows): 902 B serialized per row, so the removed
payload is ≈ 10.3 MiB at the modelled 12k assets and ≈ 103 MiB nationally — an
estimate from the measured width, not a server measurement. Median of 7 real
queries: page 1.97 ms, date-wise 1.43 ms, count 0.72 ms, overview 0.71 ms,
branch-scoped overview 1.00 ms, available 1.09 ms. Full gates green: `tsc`,
1338 tests (1336 pass, 0 fail, 2 Windows-only skips), docs-count 1338,
`no-inline-sql`, build, bundle budget 15.7 kB entry / 280.4 kB startup
(17 kB / 320 kB). Evidence: `tests/fixedAssetsOffBootstrap.test.ts` and the
updated register/refresh guards. Landed as `3efd446`; CI run
[40440447352](https://github.com/yogneoz/ISP-Inventory-Management-System/actions/runs/40440447352)
green in 368 s.

## ✅ BOOT-1 implemented — the bootstrap stops reading ledgers it never ships (2026-10-26)

**Problem.** Round 2 removed what the client receives, but the full bootstrap
still read the whole fiscal year's purchase orders, purchase invoices and stock
operations on every login. The first three fed only `computeFinancialSummary`
and were then dropped from the payload; `purchaseOrders` was fetched and never
referenced at all. It also serialized `vendorPayments`, which no client state,
type or screen reads.

**Fix.** The full bootstrap now calls `fetchFinancialSummaryFromDb` — the same
eight indexed aggregates the targeted slice and the batch report already use —
so no item JSON leaves PostgreSQL. `fetchOperationalData` dropped those four
reads and the now-unused opening-balance query: 18 fan-out queries instead of
23 (plus the eight cheap summary aggregates). `vendorPayments` left the payload
and App's PROCUREMENT refresh-domain list. Every removed key keeps its
`BOOTSTRAP_TABLES` mapping, so `GET /api/bootstrap/local?key=<key>` still serves
an older client.

**Parity.** `computeFinancialSummary` stays as the pure oracle. A new live test
rebuilds its source from the same seeded rows through the real column mappings
and compares all eight fields with the bootstrap response, so the SQL producer
is checked against the rule rather than against itself.

**Cleanup.** Deleted the stale `FRONTEND-AUDIT.md` (its P1 product-sale finding
is fixed and its G.6 split is executed); the stockops code comments that cited
it now name the decomposition directly.

**Measured** with a scratch-schema benchmark at modelled 1×/10× (four-line
operation items, real column shape):

| Volume | Rows | Row text removed | Old four-ledger read | New SQL summary |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1× | 154,000 | 63.1 MB | 1,803 ms median (1,525 best) | 187 ms median (154 best) |
| 10× | 1,540,000 | 632.4 MB | not materializable — connection dropped under the load | 1,402 ms median (1,247 best), 70 MB RSS |

The 10× old-path failure is the finding: the previous login path pulled 632 MB
of row text into Node. The scratch aggregates are unindexed, so the new times
are an upper bound. Details in
[worklist/done/2026-10-27-round-3/BOOT-1.md](../../worklist/done/2026-10-27-round-3/BOOT-1.md).

**Gates.** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; the financial-summary, bootstrap
trim/slices/FY, cache parity, returns-picker and transaction-log suites
**61/61 pass**; full suite **1255 tests, 1253 pass, 0 fail, 2 Windows-only
skips**, docs-count gate at 1255; build clean and bundle budget
**279.0 kB / 15.7 kB** (320 / 17); both convention guards green.

## ✅ VOL-3 shipped — shipments leave the bootstrap, the register pages (2026-10-26)

**Problem.** `shipments` was the last whole-ledger array in the login payload
besides customers and customer devices. The register filtered the fiscal year in
the browser and paginated the filtered copy; the shell badges, header count,
dashboard KPI, notification center and both receive lanes derived their numbers
from that same array. The old `GET /api/shipments` read every matching row and
then applied branch scope in JavaScript, so a branch account never saw foreign
rows but the server still transferred them.

**Fix.** Three reads replace the array:
- `GET /api/shipments/page` — `{ data, page, pageSize, totalItems }`, pageSize
  capped at 200, stable `(created_at, id)` order, filters for branch (either
  end), direction, status list, free text (tracking code, branch names, item
  lines), dispatch dates and warehouse destinations.
- `GET /api/shipments/overview` — exact per-status counts, every in-flight row
  (bounded at 1,000 with `pendingCapped`) and the latest 200 rows; the shell,
dashboard, notifications and receive lanes share one snapshot through
`shipmentOverviewStore.ts` / `shipmentOverviewCache.ts`.
- `GET /api/shipments` — the bare-array contract stays for the FY closing
  wizard, now bounded at 2,000 with the same filters.

Branch scope is resolved from the session and applied in SQL, so the paged and
overview reads cannot be widened either; naming a foreign branch is a 403. The
bootstrap fan-out no longer reads shipments (17 queries instead of 18) and the
payload has no `shipments` key. The `BOOTSTRAP_TABLES` mapping remains, so an
older client's local slice still answers.

**Register.** `ShipmentRegister` owns its page, uses the shared exact counts for
the KPI cards, exports a bounded snapshot of the current filters and re-runs on
a new `shipments` register-refresh key (SSE `SHIPMENTS`/`APPROVALS` and every
full refresh). `App` still holds a bounded window — every in-flight row plus the
latest 200 — so the badge/notification/receive code keeps its shape without
receiving history.

**Measured** on a TEMP table shaped like `shipments` (four-line item document,
old read vs new reads):

| Volume | Rows | Old payload | Old read | Page (25) | Overview |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1× | 20,000 | 17.2 MB / 781 ms | 0.31 MB gzip | 129 ms | 127 ms |
| 10× | 200,000 | 173.0 MB / 5,035 ms | 3.12 MB gzip | 439 ms | 1,159 ms |

The table is unindexed, so the new times are an upper bound. Details in
[worklist/done/2026-10-27-round-3/VOL-3.md](../../worklist/done/2026-10-27-round-3/VOL-3.md).

**Gates.** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; 109 targeted tests pass (page/overview over
real HTTP, repo filters, mirror twin, store scope/dedupe, bootstrap and
register-refresh guards); full suite **1286 tests, 1284 pass, 0 fail, 2
Windows-only skips**, docs-count gate at 1286; build clean and bundle budget
**279.9 kB / 15.8 kB** (320 / 17); both convention guards green.

**Landed** as `acafd52` and pushed to `main`; CI run
[40288667413](https://github.com/yogneoz/ISP-Inventory-Management-System/actions/runs/40288667413)
(`typecheck-and-test`) green in 327 s.

## ✅ VOL-4a shipped — customers leave the bootstrap, the pickers own their search (2026-10-26)

**Problem.** `customer_records` was the largest master table still shipped whole
on login: the fan-out selected every row in scope with no cap. The sale form,
the stock-out panel, the asset-assignment panel and the demo-clear card all held
the array just to resolve one record (or a count), while the pickers already
searched the server. Two national-scale defects sat behind it: the search ORed
six `LOWER(col) LIKE '%term%'` predicates (a sequential scan per debounced
keystroke), and `searchCustomers` never sent a branch — which the route guard
answers with **403**, so the shared picker was broken for every branch role.

**Fix.** `GET /api/bootstrap` has no `customers` key and the fan-out no longer
reads the table; `BOOTSTRAP_TABLES.customers` stays so an older bundle's
`/api/bootstrap/local?key=customers` still answers. Every customer choice now
comes from the paged, branch-scoped directory: the controller resolves the
session's allowed branches and applies `branch_id = ANY(...)` in SQL on top of
the route guard, and `searchCustomers` sends the shell's active branch (falling
back to the account branch). The search moved to one concatenated, lower-cased
expression behind `idx_customer_records_search_trgm`, with a
`(branch_id, customer_name, customer_id)` btree for the stable page. The hosts
keep the `CustomerRecord` the picker returned (no `.find` over an array), the
stock-out panel starts with no customer selected, the demo-clear card reads its
count from `page=1&pageSize=1`, and `AssetDeployments` lost its unused prop.

**Measured** on a TEMP `customer_records`-shaped table (Nepali name, ward-level
address, phone, username, email; 14 branches):

| Volume | Rows | Scope | Old payload | Picker (no index) | Picker (trigram) |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 1× | 12,000 | one branch (857) | 0.24 MB / 20 kB gzip | 46 ms | **5 ms** |
| 1× | 12,000 | national | **3.34 MB / 284 kB gzip** | — | — |
| 10× | 120,000 | one branch (8,571) | 2.41 MB / 192 kB gzip | 406 ms | **32 ms** |
| 10× | 120,000 | national | **33.72 MB / 2.84 MB gzip** | — | — |

`EXPLAIN` shows the bitmap index scan at both volumes; the table is unindexed
for the old path, so the comparison is the finding, not a micro-benchmark.
Details in [worklist/done/2026-10-27-round-3/VOL-4a.md](../../worklist/done/2026-10-27-round-3/VOL-4a.md).

**Gates.** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; 72 targeted tests pass; full suite
**1299 tests, 1297 pass, 0 fail, 2 Windows-only skips**, docs-count gate green;
build clean and bundle budget **279.9 kB / 15.8 kB** (320 / 17); both
convention guards green.

**Landed** as `d2c017f` and pushed to `main`; CI run
[40331014556](https://github.com/yogneoz/ISP-Inventory-Management-System/actions/runs/40331014556)
(`typecheck-and-test`) green in 363 s.

## ✅ VOL-4b shipped — customer devices leave the bootstrap; the register pages (2026-10-26)

**Problem.** `customer_device_records` is one row per deployed or rented unit,
so the last customer-scale array on `/api/bootstrap` still grew with the
customer base. The register's `GET /api/customer-devices` returned a bare array
and paged it in the browser, so every debounced keystroke transferred every
match; the warranty screen built a CPE + fixed-asset list client-side; the
directory, export screen, stock panels, shipment form, shell prop and
demo-clear card each held the same array for one count or fallback.

**Fix.** The fan-out no longer reads the table and the payload has no
`customerDevices` key; `/api/bootstrap/local?key=customerDevices` still answers
an older bundle. The register reads `GET /api/customer-devices/page`
(branch/status/text/product/date filters, `pageSize` capped at 200, stable
`created_at DESC, id DESC`) and exact per-status `/overview` counts; the
warranty directory reads `/warranty`, a SQL union of CPE and fixed assets with
the state computed and filtered in SQL (and returned with each row, so the
badge cannot disagree with the filter). The export preview pages and its CSV
asks for `all=1`: 20,000-row cap with a `truncated` flag. The directory's
assigned-hardware count is a correlated SQL subquery on its paged customer row
and the expanded row asks for that customer's bounded devices by exact
`customerId`; the stock assign/exchange panels and shipment form use the serial
register; the shell prop, App state and clear-demo array are gone. Branch scope
is re-applied in SQL from the session on every read, so omission and forgery
are both 403.

**Measured** on a TEMP table of the same shape (14 branches, Nepali names,
ward-level addresses, SN/PON/MAC, five statuses; fixed assets at 1:10):

| Volume | Rows | Old national payload | Page read | Overview | Warranty page + counts |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1× | 12,000 | **4.68 MB / 435 kB gzip** | **13.7 ms** | 2.5 ms | 35 ms |
| 10× | 120,000 | **46.95 MB / 4.05 MB gzip** | **74.6 ms** | 38.7 ms | 128 ms |

`EXPLAIN` shows the trigram bitmap scan for the serial-fragment search; the
10%-matching name search resolves through the new page btree in 1.5 / 2.1 ms.
Details in [worklist/done/2026-10-27-round-3/VOL-4b.md](../../worklist/done/2026-10-27-round-3/VOL-4b.md).

**Gates.** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; 76 targeted device/repo tests plus the
register-wiring suites pass; full suite **1322 tests, 1320 pass, 0 fail, 2
Windows-only skips**, docs-count gate green; build clean and bundle budget
**280.0 kB / 15.7 kB** (320 / 17); both convention guards green.

## ✅ BUDGET-1 decided — trim the eager registers, raise the entry guard 1 kB (2026-10-26)

**Trigger.** After VOL-2 the entry chunk was 15.6 kB of its 16 kB budget and
the startup total was 313.2 kB of 320 kB — 6.8 kB total headroom, below the
note's ~8 kB floor, and 0.4 kB from failing the entry guard.

**Finding.** Five registers were in the static graph with no reason to be:
`BranchStockTracking` (3.4 kB gzip), `FixedAssetRegister` (4.5),
`CustomersManagement` (12.1), `CustomerMasterDirectory` (6.9) and
`SerialLogRegister` (6.9) — **33.8 kB gzip** of screens no first paint renders.
Dashboard, ProductManagement, StockOperations and PurchaseOrders stayed eager
as designed; every comparable register is already lazy.

**Decision.** Move those five behind `React.lazy` (named, because first visit
now fetches a chunk behind the existing fallback) and raise the entry budget
16 → 17 kB for the shell/routing, which legitimately grew from 11.7 kB in
2026-09. The 320 kB total is unchanged. Result: **15.7 kB entry / 279.0 kB
total**, **1.3 kB entry headroom and 41.0 kB total headroom**, 69 startup
chunks / 39 on-demand. The undated “entry 11.7 / total ~262” comment in the
budget script is replaced with the dated measurement and rationale. Details in
[worklist/done/2026-10-26-round-2/BUDGET-1.md](../../worklist/done/2026-10-26-round-2/BUDGET-1.md).

**Gates.** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; full `npm test` **1254 tests, 1252 pass,
0 fail, 2 Windows-only skips**; build clean; `check:bundle-budget` green;
both convention guards green. The commit is a separate step.

national volume (12k customers at 1×, 120k at 10×, Nepal-wide) that is the

refresh.

**Fix.** The Returns form now uses the shared `SearchPicker` against
`GET /api/sales-invoices?query=…&page=1&pageSize=20`: search by invoice number
or customer name, full row (including `items`) back, same `applyInvoice` /
maximum-quantity / already-returned prefill path. The Sales register lost its

on screen plus the existing error + Retry. `salesInvoices` left

render sites keep their `purchaseInvoices` slice untouched.

using the B1 benchmark's documented invoice volumes. Details in

## ✅ VOL-1 implemented — the movement log is one scoped, paged read (2026-10-26)

**Problem.** `transactionLogs` rode the bootstrap company-wide for the fiscal
year with no row cap. The Dashboard rendered eight rows; the Audit Trail paged
the whole array in the browser; the movement ledger merged it client-side. At
the intended national volume (well past 12k customers, Nepal-wide) that is
hundreds of thousands of rows on every login and every full refresh.

**Fix.** `GET /api/transaction-logs` now has a paged envelope
(`{ data, page, pageSize, totalItems, capped }`), an inclusive AD date range,
`order=asc|desc` and `all=1` capped at 5,000 for export/ledger reads. The legacy
bare array still answers a request with no page params, capped at 2,000. Branch
scope is resolved on the server: omitting `branchId` means "my allowed
branches", another branch is `403`, and `branchId=ALL` from a branch account is
`403`. Dashboard reads eight rows; Audit Trail reads 15/page with a capped
export notice; the movement ledger reads one bounded FY/branch window (5,000)
and merges the operation/damage arrays once, stating the cap. A true three-way
server union is recorded as the follow-up when `stock_operations` leaves the
bootstrap. `transactionLog` is now the eighth paged-register counter, bumped by
`STOCK`, `STOCK_OPERATIONS`, `PROCUREMENT`, `SALES`, `SHIPMENTS`,
`CUSTOMER_DEVICES`, `ASSETS` and `RECALC`.

**Measured.** A 20,000-row sample in the real projection costs **441 B raw /
44.4 B gzip per row**. Removed from the login payload: **42.06 MB raw / 4.23 MB
gzip at 1× (100k movements/year)** and **420.60 MB raw / 42.33 MB gzip at 10×
(1M/year)**. The live demo bootstrap is 0.95 MB raw / 0.06 MB gzip and no longer
has the key. Details in [worklist/done/2026-10-26-round-2/VOL-1.md](../../worklist/done/2026-10-26-round-2/VOL-1.md).

**Gates.** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; full `npm test` **1247 tests, 1245 pass,
0 fail, 2 Windows-only skips**; `npm run build` clean; `check:bundle-budget`
**313.2 kB** startup / 15.7 kB entry (320 / 16); `check:no-inline-sql` and
`check:no-raw-dialogs` green. The commit is a separate step.

## ✅ SUM-1 shipped — financial-summary trading totals aggregate in SQL (2026-10-26)

**Problem.** `GET /api/bootstrap/local?key=financialSummary` refreshes on seven
SSE domains. Each refresh selected every `STOCK_OUT` operation's `items` JSON
for the fiscal year/branch, parsed every line in Node, and resolved each line's
product cost with a `products.find`. At the modelled 10× volume that was
hundreds of thousands of line objects shipped and re-parsed to produce two
numbers.

**Fix.** [buildTradingTotalsQuery](../../server/src/models/reports.repo.ts) aggregates
revenue and COGS inside PostgreSQL with `jsonb_to_recordset`, joining
`products` on `p.id::text` so a malformed line can never fail a uuid cast. The
rule matches `computeTradingFromOps`: `totalValue` when non-zero, otherwise
`max(0, qty × sellingPrice − discount)`; COGS is `qty × (unitCost || product
costPrice || 0)` for positive quantities; lines with neither price field are
skipped. `NULLIF(..., 0)` preserves the JS fallback. The slice now issues eight
indexed reads and returns two numbers; the pure rule stays for the full
bootstrap and [tradingTotals.test.ts](../../tests/tradingTotals.test.ts) pins the two
producers to the same parity matrix, including another branch and an
out-of-range row.

**Measured** on an isolated 12k/120k-row scratch schema with four-line sales,
seven runs each:

| Volume | Old payload | Old wall + Node parse | New wall | New p95 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 12k (1×) | 2.7 MiB | 86 ms + 17 ms ≈ 103 ms | 108 ms | 113 ms |
| 120k (10×) | 27.4 MiB | 750 ms + 74 ms ≈ 824 ms | 357 ms | 406 ms |

The first SQL attempt (`jsonb_array_elements` with a cast per field) was slower
than the old path; the recordset replaced it. At 1× p95 is slightly better than
the old total; at 10× it is ~2.3× faster and returns one row instead of 27.4
MiB. DB CPU is the new cost, so a write-time total is the recorded fallback if
that becomes the constraint — B3 stays parked.

**Gates.** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; targeted summary/repo/parity tests green;
full `npm test` green (two Windows-only skips); `check:no-inline-sql` and
`check:no-raw-dialogs` clean. No production client change.

## ✅ TRIM-1 shipped — quick search is one bounded, scoped server read (2026-10-25)

**Problem.** The global search modal received whole bootstrap slices (products,
POs, PIs, shipments, fixed assets, customer devices, stock) and filtered them on
every keystroke. That is bounded only by how much data the install holds — and
this install is expected to grow well past 12k customers and cover Nepal-wide
territory, so both the startup payload and the per-keystroke scan scale with the
business. It was also the last screen filtering a whole deferred slice
client-side.

**Fix.** `GET /api/global-search?q=&branchId=` returns **at most five rows per
group**, only the fields the modal renders, in six independent `LIMIT`ed reads.
Query length is clamped to 2–80 characters and LIKE wildcards are escaped.
Scope is a server decision: non-global roles are pinned to their own branch (no
`branchId` cannot widen it; another branch is `403`), global roles may pass
`ALL`, and `x-fiscal-year-id` scopes the document/asset/device groups.
Indexes: POs/PIs reuse the B2 `search_text` projection; four new trigram GIN
expression indexes (products, shipments, assets, customer devices) exactly match
the query expressions. Demo mode returns the same bounded shape from the
in-memory arrays. The modal debounces 250 ms, drops superseded responses, and
renders loading / retryable error / empty as distinct states. Suppliers and
branches stay local: they are the two small reference tables the shell already
needs.

**Also hardened (found while running the full suite).** The SER-2 reconcile was
written before SER-1: it mapped every stock operation to `IN_STOCK`, so a
maintenance pass could relist a `SOLD`/`CONSUMED` unit or move a received pullout
back to its source, and one orphan product id in old document JSON aborted the
whole pass on the `serial_log` FK. It now mirrors `issueStatusForOperation`
(`SOLD`/`CONSUMED`/`DAMAGED`/`IN_TRANSIT`, received pullout → `IN_STOCK` at its
destination, `CANCELLED` skipped, `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT` moves no serial), resolves
stale product/branch links at write time, counts `skipped` for a reference that
still loses the race, and compares-and-sets on the status it read so a live
receive/sale/deploy mid-pass is never overwritten. `skipped` is reported in the
endpoint response and audit event.

**Files.** [globalSearch.repo.ts](../../server/src/models/globalSearch.repo.ts),
[globalSearch.controller.ts](../../server/src/controllers/globalSearch.controller.ts),
[search.ts](../../client/src/services/api/search.ts),
[GlobalSearchModal.tsx](../../client/src/components/common/GlobalSearchModal.tsx),
[AppShell.tsx](../../client/src/components/layout/AppShell.tsx),
[schema.sql](../../scripts/schema.sql), [serialBackfill.service.ts](../../server/src/services/serialBackfill.service.ts),
[admin.controller.ts](../../server/src/controllers/admin.controller.ts).

**Tests (10 new; suite 1236).** [globalSearch.api.test.ts](../../tests/globalSearch.api.test.ts)
(5, real HTTP + PostgreSQL) and [globalSearchContract.test.ts](../../tests/globalSearchContract.test.ts)
(5 source guards). The SER-2 suite gained lifecycle and orphan-product cases in
[serialRegisterBackfill.test.ts](../../tests/serialRegisterBackfill.test.ts); the
no-inline-SQL seeded fixtures now live in the OS temp dir, so a concurrent guard
run can no longer scan them.

**Verification.** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `npm test`: 1236 tests, 1234 pass, 0
fail, 2 unconditional Windows signal skips; docs-count gate green at 1236.
`npm run build` clean; `check:bundle-budget` **312.8 kB** startup / 15.7 kB entry
(budgets 320 / 16) — the search store is server-side, so no client growth.
`check:no-inline-sql` and `check:no-raw-dialogs` green.

## ✅ D4 assessed — A5 deferred, with measurements (2026-10-25)

A5 is the operational-data store. Its gate, D4, asked whether root re-renders or
prop drilling are still user-visible after A1 (screen element memoisation), A3
(session/scope contexts) and the targeted-SSE slice work.

**Measured.** The root holds 23 operational arrays across 35 `useState`; the
67-entry route table threads 756 prop passings (144 distinct, 236 handlers) and
has **zero** derived-per-render props. With the real App mounted through the A6
harness (4 tabs open, 120 stock rows, Profiler around `<App>`): a targeted STOCK
SSE update commits ~298 ms total / 129 ms max and a full refresh ~381 ms total —
the cost sits in the list screens that genuinely consume the changed rows, which
a store would also re-render. The root's own work — building all 67 elements
with ~11 props each and comparing the memo cache — is **2.1 ms per render**
(Node micro-benchmark). A1 reuses the element object for any screen whose props
are unchanged, so an unrelated mutation does not re-render mounted screens.

**Decision.** Defer A5. The remaining debt (756 prop passings, ~100-value deps
contract) is maintainability, not measured jank, and A5 is a week of high-risk
churn. A4b stays blocked, as the backlog already requires. Reopen A5 only if a
real-browser profile shows root commits above the 16 ms frame budget, or if
A4b's typed deps contract becomes the priority. Recorded in
[docs/architecture-backlog.md](../../docs/architecture-backlog.md)
(the temporary worklist that tracked it has been deleted now the pass is closed).
**Confirmed in a real browser (same day).** The production build (`node dist/server.cjs`, port 3123, logged in as the demo SUPER_ADMIN) mounted six tabs, then a reorder-level PATCH on a real stock row drove exactly the four targeted STOCK slice fetches — `stock`, `damageRecords`, `transactionLogs`, `financialSummary` — with no full `/api/bootstrap` call. `PerformanceObserver('longtask')` recorded **zero** long tasks during both the idle baseline and the update window, and the rAF cadence did not change (median 31.2 ms, max 31.6 ms vs a 31.7 ms baseline at the headless 30 Hz cadence). The mutated reorder level was restored to 40; the temporary server and log were removed. The deferral therefore stands on production-build evidence, not only jsdom.

No production code changed for this assessment.

## ✅ SER-2 shipped — the serial register reconciles on demand, boot only points at it (2026-10-25)

**Decision D3 (recorded first).** Keep the converge pass, remove the boot call. A
boot-time pass made the register converge silently: a restart created rows for
units nobody had registered and upgraded transient states without an operator
asking. The pass is now an explicit SUPER_ADMIN action, idempotent, reporting
inserted/upgraded counts. Historical rows are untouched by the move; a legacy
database runs the action once.

**Fix.** [serialBackfill.service.ts](../../server/src/services/serialBackfill.service.ts)
owns the merge (five legacy sources → one row per serial, terminal states never
downgraded) and returns `{ converged, inserted, upgraded }` instead of logging.
`POST /api/admin/recalculate/serial-register` is SUPER_ADMIN-only (the route sits
under the global `/admin/` role rule as well), writes a `BACKFILL_SERIAL_REGISTER`
audit event, and answers `503` when PostgreSQL is down rather than pretending to
have converged. Settings → Data Recalculation & Maintenance gains the
**Reconcile Serial Register** card. [dbBoot.ts](../../server/src/boot/dbBoot.ts) no
longer imports or calls the pass; it logs where the action lives.

**Tests (6 new; suite 1236).** [tests/serialRegisterBackfill.test.ts](../../tests/serialRegisterBackfill.test.ts):
source guard (boot never calls/imports the pass and keeps the pointer log; the
route carries `requireRole('SUPER_ADMIN')`), real-PostgreSQL convergence (invoice
→ `IN_STOCK`/`PURCHASE`, damage op → `DAMAGED`, customer device →
`CUSTOMER_ASSIGNED`), idempotency (no duplicates, stable rows on re-run), terminal
preservation (`RETURNED` survives a pass), and HTTP gating (401 anonymous, 403
ACCOUNTANT, 200 SUPER_ADMIN).

**Verification.** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; the six new cases pass against
PostgreSQL 16; the full suite and the docs-count gate are green.

## ✅ SER-1 shipped — pulled-out, issued and sold units leave the register (2026-10-25)

**Decisions D2/D3 (recorded first).** `PULLOUT` moves a unit `IN_STOCK →
IN_TRANSIT` at the source; receiving the dispatch lands it `IN_STOCK` at the
destination. `CONSUMABLE_ISSUE` moves `IN_STOCK → CONSUMED` (new state) and
`STOCK_OUT` (product sale) moves `IN_STOCK → SOLD`. Every transition records the
operation as `source_type='STOCK_OP'` / `source_id` plus a history entry, and
every transition has a symmetric reversal back to `IN_STOCK`. Historical rows are
deliberately **not** auto-mapped: an old operation cannot distinguish a unit
returned outside the operation, so only new transitions are tracked. D3 retires
the boot backfill only in SER-2 (explicit SUPER_ADMIN maintenance action; no
silent convergence), so `dbBoot` is unchanged here.

**Fix.** [serialOperationLifecycle.service.ts](../../server/src/services/serialOperationLifecycle.service.ts)
owns the mapping, DB transitions and in-memory twins. `post_stockOperations`
issues the gate-resolved units inside its transaction; `post_receive` lands a
received pullout at the destination; `post_reverseConsumable` restores `CONSUMED`
units. New `POST /api/stock-operations/:id/reverse-pullout` and
`/reverse-stock-out` reverse their own operation (damaged-stock pullout lines
return to the damaged pool), using the same permission as the operation they
undo. The logs panel exposes the sale reversal and the pullout panel exposes the
dispatch reversal. `serial_log` gains `CONSUMED` (fresh check + boot migration);
the register vocabulary, status cards and filter carry it.

**Tests.** `tests/serialOperationLifecycle.test.ts` pins the mapping, the
damaged-line exclusion, branch/status scoping, reverse-only-own-units and the
receive move. `tests/serialGateHttp.test.ts` adds real-HTTP cases: a pullout
becomes `IN_TRANSIT`, a re-issue is refused, reversal restores both serial and
stock; consumable `CONSUMED` and sale `SOLD` reverse correctly; the wrong
reversal endpoint is refused; a received pullout moves to the destination, and
reversing the dispatch after that receipt is refused (`400`) with both branches'
stock and the unit's branch unchanged — otherwise the source would be credited
again while the unit sits at the destination.

**Verification.** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. The full run at the time was 1236
tests against a real PostgreSQL 16 service container, 2 Windows-only skips,
0 fail; the docs counts gate was green for that run.

## ✅ CPE-3 shipped — the fixed asset follows the CPE lifecycle, once per asset (2026-10-25)

**Decision D1 (recorded first).** A CPE collection returns the linked fixed
asset to `ACTIVE` with every assignment field and `device_serial` cleared, keeps
the prior assignment in `assignment_notes`, and credits one unit of branch stock
only when the deployment proved a `STOCK_OUT` for that product/tag. The credit
is keyed on the fixed-asset id, so the approval path, the direct status PATCH
and the asset-unassign screen cannot each add a unit. An exchange keeps the tag
and customer, moves `device_serial` to the replacement, records the old → new
serial, and posts the replacement's `STOCK_OUT` in the same transaction as the
old unit's disposition (RESTOCK / DAMAGE / DISPOSED).

**Fix.** [cpeAssetLifecycle.service.ts](../../server/src/services/cpeAssetLifecycle.service.ts)
is the single owner of that rule. `misc.controller` (approval execution),
`inventory.controller` `patch_status` (asset unassign) and `patch_status2`
(collection statuses), and `post_exchange` all call it inside their existing
transactions. New repo SQL locks the asset by id or by serial/tag, applies the
collect/move, proves the deployment, and resolves the replacement's product;
`STOCK_RETURN_FROM_ASSET_SQL` now returns the new quantity so the ledger's
before/after are the committed truth. Legacy CPE rows with no linked asset are
keyed by the CPE row id (request number as fallback), so approval execution and
a direct status PATCH also agree. Failure paths now restore the in-memory row and map
`OperationError` (for example the empty-replacement-stock `409`) instead of
answering a generic 500. `scripts/schema.sql` now accepts the assignment
statuses the deploy/lifecycle paths write: the original `fixed_assets` status
check only listed `ACTIVE`/`DISPOSED`/`WRITTEN_OFF`/`MAINTENANCE`, so a fresh or
upgraded database rejected every `ASSIGNED_TO_CUSTOMER` asset. The migration
drops and re-creates `fixed_assets_status_check` on boot, and a scratch database
built from the new schema accepted the assignment row.

**Tests.** `tests/cpeAssetLifecycle.test.ts` on real PostgreSQL: collect once,
collect twice (no second credit), unlinked collection keyed by its CPE row, exchange
moves serial and deducts the replacement, empty replacement stock refuses and
rolls back, a forced failure rolls back asset + stock + ledger, and
exchange→collect ends `ACTIVE` with stock balanced. The CPE status-PATCH HTTP
suite now seeds a linked asset and asserts the patch returns it to `ACTIVE`
with one asset-keyed credit; the exchange concurrency suite and the MAC-conflict
HTTP suite were updated to expect the replacement's stock-out.

**Verification.** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean and the affected suites green. The
`npm test` run at that head executed 1210 tests against a real PostgreSQL 16
service container, 1208 pass, 2 Windows-only skips, 0 fail (the then-current
header carried the same count).

## ✅ B2 shipped — one trigram projection serves all eight item searches (2026-10-25)

**Problem.** B1 measured eight `items::text` substring scans (five register
predicates plus three serial-rename pre-selects). At 120k rows four took more
than a second (1.25–4.09 s), and the register predicates ORed header columns
with the JSONB blob, so an items-only index could not be used: an unindexed
header branch forced a sequential scan.

**Fix.** `scripts/schema.sql` now creates `pg_trgm` and gives each searched
table one stored generated projection, `search_text`, holding the lower-cased
concatenation of exactly the columns its old predicate searched:

| Table | Folded into `search_text` |
|---|---|
| `purchase_orders` | `po_number`, `supplier_name`, `items` |
| `purchase_invoices` | `invoice_number`, `vendor_bill_number`, `supplier_name`, `items` |
| `shipments` | `items` |
| `stock_operations` | `reference_number`, `technician_name`, `work_order_ref`, `reason`, `items` |
| `purchase_returns` | `return_number`, `original_invoice_number`, `supplier_name`, `items` |
| `sales_returns` | `return_number`, `original_invoice_number`, `customer_name`, `items` |

Each table gets a `gin (search_text gin_trgm_ops)` index. `sales_invoices`
carries `items` but has no item-search query, so it is deliberately excluded.
The runtime parity block adds every column with
`ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS … GENERATED … STORED` before the
`IF NOT EXISTS` indexes, so an existing database upgrades on its next boot and
a fresh install declares the columns in `CREATE TABLE` directly.

All eight call sites now emit one lower-cased `search_text LIKE $n` instead of
the column-by-column OR: `buildPurchaseOrderWhere`,
`buildPurchaseInvoiceWhere`, `buildPurchaseReturnWhere`,
`buildStockOperationWhere`, `buildSalesReturnWhere`, and the three-table serial
pre-select in `cascadeRenameJsonbTables` (its parameters are lower-cased to
match the generated column). `grep -rn 'items::text' server/src` is now empty.

**Verification.** Targeted repo/serial/schema tests are green, followed by the
full suite: **1203 tests, 1201 pass, 2 skipped, 0 fail**, docs-count gate green
at 1203. A fresh scratch database (`inventory_bench_b2`, schema-only, then
12k seeded stock operations) measured the real stock-operation count: at 12k
rows the old predicate and the projection both used a Seq Scan (54.7 ms →
17.9 ms); after growing to 120k rows the old predicate stayed on a Seq Scan at
**265.2 ms**, while `search_text LIKE` switched to a **Bitmap Heap Scan at
15.4 ms** and returned the same single row. That is the B1 shape reproduced
against the shipped schema, not a replay of B1's richer four-object dataset.
The upgrade path was exercised separately: a database built from the pre-B2
`HEAD` schema was seeded with 12k stock operations, then the new schema was
applied. All six columns and six indexes appeared, the same serial search
still matched exactly one row, a second apply was a clean no-op, and the count
used a Bitmap Heap Scan at 9.5 ms; the full first apply took ~4.7 s at 12k
rows, so treat the generated-column rewrite as a one-time maintenance cost that
grows with table size.

**Where.** `scripts/schema.sql` (extension, six columns, six indexes, runtime
ALTERs), `server/src/models/{procurement,inventory,sales}.repo.ts`,
`server/src/services/serials.service.ts`, and the repo/serial/schema tests.
Backlog §B2 now records the delivered shape and numbers.

## ✅ B1 measured — item search degrades with rows; B2 is a GO on the combined expression (2026-10-25)

**Result.** Eight `items::text` scans were measured with
`EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` on scratch database `inventory_bench_b1`
(PostgreSQL 18.6, `scripts/schema.sql` unchanged) at a modelled 1× year and
10×: 4k/40k purchase orders, 4k/40k purchase invoices, 6k/60k shipments,
12k/120k stock operations, 12k/120k sales invoices, 600/6k purchase returns,
900/9k sales returns. At 10× four scans exceeded one second: stock operations
1.25 s, serial rename PI 2.51 s, shipments 4.09 s and stock operations 2.88 s.
The 1× stock-operation scan already took 302 ms and the three serial scans
250–759 ms. Register page queries tracked the scan (SO 293 ms at 1×, 1.22 s at
10×), and a register request runs the same `WHERE` about three times, so the
10× stock-operation screen pays ~3.7 s of scans before rendering. A second full
run varied individual p50s but kept the ranking and every >1 s result.

**Decision.** **B2 GO; B3/B4 parked.** A bare `items::text` trigram GIN is not
enough: the five register predicates OR the items field with unindexed header
columns, and a scratch spot check confirmed the planner kept a 284.6 ms seq
scan. A GIN on the combined
`lower(coalesce(header…,'') || ' ' || … || items::text)` expression used a
Bitmap Index Scan and ran the same 12k-row scan in 5.5 ms. B2 shipped that
expression as a generated stored column in `schema.sql` and now points all
eight scans at it (see the B2 entry above). Threshold recorded: p95 > 150 ms at
modelled 1× volume, or a >50k-row sequential scan at 10×.

**Where.** Full dataset, 1×/10× tables, plan shapes, reproducible SQL and
variance are in `docs/architecture-backlog.md` §B1; the follow-up plan then pointed
at B2, which is now delivered. This B1 slice itself made no schema or application
change. Targeted docs-count guard green (8/8); a docs-only slice does not need
the full suite.

## ✅ CPE-2 shipped — MAC conflicts are a readable 409 and the dead status vocabulary is gone (2026-10-25)

**Problem.** `CDR_DUPLICATE_CHECK_SQL` checked `device_serial` and
`pon_serial` only. A MAC owned by another row reached
`uq_customer_device_mac_address` and surfaced as a raw `500` from create or
exchange. Separately, `DISCONNECTED` and `RETURNED` were still accepted as new
values even though the live flow writes `ROUTER_COLLECTED` for a collection and
`REFUND`/`IN_STOCK` for a return.

**Fix.** The duplicate probe now covers a non-empty `mac_address` by its
canonical 12-hex key (so `a4c3f0b1d2e3` collides with
`A4:C3:F0:B1:D2:E3`, while non-MAC text is compared literally) and returns the
matched field. Create and exchange probe before any write and return `409`
with a message naming the field; `findCdrDuplicate` is the single rule shared by
the PG probe, the demo mirror and the exchange pre-flight. The exchange now
hydrates a blank PON/MAC from the register **before** the probe — previously a
serial-only payload skipped the MAC half and could still reach the unique
index.

**Vocabulary count.** A live query found **zero** rows in `DISCONNECTED` or
`RETURNED` (and no approval rows requesting either), so both values were pruned
from every write path: create and status PATCH refuse them with a readable
`400`, approval submission and execution refuse a legacy requested status
before mutating anything, and the UI no longer offers or special-cases them.
The type union and read path keep the values so a pre-cleanup row, if one is
ever imported, remains readable; the live vocabulary is `ROUTER_COLLECTED`,
`REFUND` and `IN_STOCK`.

**Tests.** `tests/cdrDuplicates.test.ts` (7 unit tests: canonical MAC, serial/PON
case and trim, empties, self-exclusion, 409 copy, PG-row mapping) and
`tests/cpeMacConflict.test.ts` (7 HTTP cases against real PostgreSQL:
duplicate create, new create, legacy PATCH, legacy approval submission,
duplicate exchange, register-hydrated duplicate exchange, successful exchange).
`tests/inventory.repo.test.ts` pins the probe SQL, and
`tests/cdrSerialSync.test.ts` pins that legacy statuses no longer map into
`serial_log`. Full suite green; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.

## ✅ CPE-1 shipped — the CPE status PATCH has a manager audience (2026-10-25)

**Problem.** `PATCH /api/customer-devices/:id/status` had no role gate. The
global operational-permission middleware has no `customer-devices` rule, so any
authenticated role that could reach the branch could flip a rental to
`REFUND`/`IN_STOCK` and rewrite its `serial_log` status without the approval
workflow. The app-level branch middleware also cannot resolve `req.params.id`
before Express routes the request, so it did not protect this id-only path.

**Fix.** The route now uses `requireRole(...CPE_STATUS_ROLES)`: the shared CPE
manager base (`SUPER_ADMIN`, `HEAD_OFFICE_ADMIN`, `BRANCH_MANAGER`) plus
`INVENTORY_MANAGER`. The exchange lookup/exchange/refusal routes use the same
base plus `FIELD_TECHNICIAN`, so the two audiences cannot drift. The status
handler re-checks the record's branch before touching either the mirror or
PostgreSQL and returns `403` for a branch-bound actor outside that branch.
CPE-1 deliberately left status normalization (`DISCONNECTED` →
`ROUTER_COLLECTED`) and the audit event unchanged; CPE-2 later removed that dead
alias from the write path.

**Tests.** `tests/cpeStatusAuthz.test.ts` (4 HTTP cases against real
PostgreSQL): no token → 401; `FRONT_DESK` (which holds `customers-manage` but
must use approval) → 403 with both rows unchanged; an allowed `BRANCH_MANAGER`
against another branch's record → 403 with both rows unchanged; and
`INVENTORY_MANAGER` → 200, `REFUND` on the customer-device row and `IN_STOCK`
with the customer attribution cleared on `serial_log`. The exchange audience pin
now asserts the shared constant keeps `FIELD_TECHNICIAN`.

**Verification.** The suite was green on that head: the `npm test` run against a
real PostgreSQL 16 service container executed 1203 tests, 1201 pass, 2
Windows-only skips, 0 fail; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.

## ✅ Bootstrap trim follow-up — KPI counts, open-PO picker and FY wizard read scoped rows (2026-10-25)

**Problem.** The deferred purchase slices stopped shipping in the full
bootstrap in October, but the dashboard tile, sidebar badges, notification
panel, vendor-bill PO picker and FY closing wizard still received the whole
`purchaseOrders` / `purchaseInvoices` / `shipments` arrays from
`/api/bootstrap/local`. That kept the byte cost and meant every one of those
surfaces re-filtered the same fiscal-year ledger on the client.

**Fix.** Three consumers moved to purpose-scoped reads:

1. **KPI counts.** New `services/procurementPendingStore.ts` (pure,
   scope-keyed) + `procurementPendingCache.ts` (React hook + paged fetch) load
   only open POs (`DRAFT/APPROVED/SENT/IN_PROGRESS/PURCHASED`) and the unpaid
   bill count (`totalItems − PAID`) for the header branch + selected FY. One
   shared request feeds the sidebar badges, dashboard tile and notification
   panel; a scope switch clears the previous branch/FY before the new read, a
   failed read keeps the last good counts, and App shows a Retry notice. PO/PI
   mutations and shipment receipts invalidate the cache from the SSE handler
   and `refreshAllData`; sign-out resets it. App imports the cache dynamically
   after first paint so the new store does not grow the startup chunk (the
   bundle budget had failed by 2.3 kB; now 311.9 kB of the 320 kB budget).
2. **Vendor-bill PO picker.** `PurchaseInvoiceForm` no longer takes the whole
   `purchaseOrders` array: it reads a **separate** scoped store instance keyed
   by the bill's own destination branch + fiscal year, so the link dialog lists
   only open POs for the branch/FY the bill is being written in, with a
   loading/error notice and Retry inside the dialog. A separate instance is
   deliberate: reusing the KPI store would let a destination-branch change in
   the form rewrite the shell's header-branch badge scope. Both stores reset on
   sign-out and invalidate on the same procurement/shipment events.
3. **FY closing wizard.** It now loads its own POs, vendor bills and shipments
   for the fiscal year being closed (its selected year can differ from the
   header FY), on tab activation and on procurement/shipment events. It keeps
   the last loaded documents for that FY on failure, shows a Retry notice and a
   synthetic "Fiscal-Year Purchase Documents: Unavailable" diagnostic, and
   blocks Step 1's Next while the read is loading or failed — instead of
   reporting an unverified year as "Ready". It also lost the string-summing
   VAT bug the scoped rows exposed (`"02925.00" + "16250.00"`), now coerced.

**Server.** `GET /api/purchase-orders` accepts a comma-separated `status`
(one value keeps the old `status = $n` shape; several become
`status = ANY($n::text[])`), and `GET /api/shipments` accepts optional
`branchId` / `dateFromAD` / `dateToAD` (either end of the transfer + dispatch
date range). Both builders and the wizard now have repo/API tests. No route
gating changed: these are the same authenticated GETs the deferred bootstrap
slices used, with the same branch/FY scope the old props carried; the client
still filters the ALL-branch dashboard/alerts through the user's allowed
branches.

**Tests.** `tests/procurementPendingStore.test.ts` (8): scope-key identity,
open-status filtering, unpaid-count aggregation, one-request dedupe, abandoned
scope responses never paint, failure keeps last-good rows, Retry clears the
error, invalidation refetches only with a subscriber, and no request without a
FY range. `tests/procurement.repo.test.ts` + `tests/shipments.repo.test.ts` cover
the new WHERE shapes. `tests/registerRefreshDomains.test.ts` pins the wizard's
three scoped calls + activation/SSE/Retry wiring and the form's cache read.
Suite **1184 tests, 1182 pass, 2 Windows-only skips, 0 fail**; `npx tsc --noEmit`
clean.

**Live verification (real PostgreSQL, running app).** ALL branches: dashboard
1 Pending PO, sidebar total 3 (1 PO + 2 unpaid bills). WH001: 1 PO + 1 bill →
badge 2; BRH01: 0 POs + 1 bill → badge 1. Vendor-bill dialog listed the one
open WH001 PO; switching the form's destination branch to BRH01 emptied the
picker (BRH01 has none) while the header badge stayed at 2 — the picker's
separate store never re-scoped the KPI counts. The wizard's Step 1 showed "1 Open" for the current FY and VAT
19,175 across 2 bills. Forced KPI failure: banner with Retry appeared and the
badge kept its last value; Retry cleared it. Forced wizard failure: Retry
notice, cached "1 Open", `Unavailable` diagnostic and disabled Next; Retry
restored the diagnostics. Network log showed only the scoped requests
(`status=…&dateFrom…&pageSize=1`, `all=1` for the wizard, shipment range).

**Scope note.** The deferred slices remain in `/api/bootstrap/local` and still
feed `StockMovementLedger` (pinned no-fetch), global search, the PO/PI
registers' prop-driven read-only bits and other wholesale consumers; these
three were the remaining per-consumer migrations named in the earlier note.

## ✅ A4a shipped — a typed route table behind AppRoutes (2026-10-25)

**Problem.** App rendered 67 `<KeepMounted>` blocks inline, each repeating the
same `visible[...]` wiring, and kept the tab permission table in a root-local
constant. The render tree and the access rule were two declarations of the same
tabs.

**Fix.** `routes/appRoutes.tsx` defines `AppRoute` and `screenRoute(key,
element)`, which attaches the RBAC operation(s) and the Super-Admin flag from
the new `routes/tabPermissions.ts`; `AppRoutes` renders the table through
`KeepMountedRoot`/`KeepMounted` with identical never-mount/hide semantics. App
builds one 67-entry `screenRoutes` array and renders `<AppRoutes … />`; its
local `TAB_PERMISSIONS`/`SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY_TABS` copies are gone and
`canAccessTab` delegates to `isTabAccessible`.

**Scope note.** This is A4a, not all of A4: the element construction still
lives in App, because moving it out needs a typed deps contract for ~100
props/handlers that A5's store owns. A4 stays unchecked in the backlog with
that remainder stated.

**Verified.** `tests/appRouteRegistry.test.ts` (5 tests): the table is declared
once with 67 unique keys and every key is a real NavTab; the permission tables
moved and each entry carries them; AppRoutes is the only route-level
keep-mounted renderer; an `enabled: false` route never mounts even when active;
and switching routes mounts each once, hides the previous one and reuses the
node on return. A6's route-tree test still passes through the registry;
`npx tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite **1184 tests, 1182 pass, 2 Windows-only
skips, 0 fail**.

## ✅ A3 shipped — session and scope have one owner (2026-10-25)

**Problem.** App.tsx held the signed-in user, root user, RBAC matrix, app
settings, date mode, selected branch, selected fiscal year and the fiscal-year
list in its own `useState`. Every root refactor (A4's route registry, screen
extraction) had to thread nine values and their setters through props, which is
why the root kept growing instead of shrinking.

**Fix.** `contexts/SessionContext.tsx` owns the five session values and
`contexts/ScopeContext.tsx` the two scope values plus the fiscal-year list; both
expose typed hooks that throw outside their provider. `App` reads them
(`useSession()` / `useScope()`) and keeps no state for any of them.
`contexts/AppProviders.tsx` is the one provider composition, used by `main.tsx`
and by the A6 route-tree harness, so the test can never miss a provider the
browser has. Screens keep their props for this slice; a guard fails if a
feature screen imports either context, preventing a half-migration.

**Verified.** `tests/sessionScopeContexts.test.ts` (5 tests): App imports and
calls both hooks with no `useState` for the nine values; each provider owns its
values and its hook fails loudly outside the provider; main and the harness
share `AppProviders`; no file under `client/src/features` imports the contexts;
and a mounted probe reads seeded values then updates date mode, branch,
fiscal-year list and app settings through the providers. A6's route-tree test
still passes through the shared composition; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; full
suite **1168 tests, 1166 pass, 2 Windows-only skips, 0 fail**.

## ✅ A6 shipped — a route-tree smoke test, not only source pins (2026-10-25)

**Problem.** Every client fix since DF-13 is pinned by reading source text,
because there was no way to mount the real route tree under `node:test`. That
is exactly what makes A3/A4 (session/scope contexts, route registry) risky: a
refactor that remounts a screen loses drafts, scroll positions and fetched
chunks, and nothing behavioural would notice.

**Fix.** `tests/harness/appRouteTreeProbe.tsx` mounts the real `App` inside the
providers `main.tsx` uses (`DarkModeProvider` + `DialogProvider`) under jsdom,
seeds a SUPER_ADMIN session, and replaces the `api` singleton's mount-time
methods with fixtures — no network, no module mocking. A fake `EventSource`
covers the SSE subscription. `tests/appRouteTree.test.ts` drives navigation
through the app's own `NAVIGATE_TAB_EVENT` (the toast "open tab" path), types a
draft into Assign Fixed Asset's product search, switches to the Dashboard and
back, then asserts the SAME DOM node is still there with the value intact. A
remount replaces the node, so the assertion fails. `App.tsx`'s
`import.meta.env.DEV` reads were made node-test-safe (the same `?.` guard
Sidebar already used) and `tests/harness/dom.ts` now exposes jsdom's
`localStorage` / `sessionStorage` globals.

**Verified.** `tests/appRouteTree.test.ts` (1 test) passes with zero
`act(...)` warnings; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite **1163 tests, 1161
pass, 2 Windows-only skips, 0 fail**. The test is picked up by `npm test`'s
existing `tests/*.test.ts` glob, so CI runs it with no new script — A6's
done-when. It proves the hidden-not-unmounted contract; each screen's data
freshness stays with that screen's own tests/guards.

## ✅ U-2 shipped — one searchable control for the large directories (2026-10-25)

**Problem.** U-1 fixed the customer field on three forms, but four screens were
still rendering an entire directory as native `<option>`s: `CustomersManagement`
and `CustomerLedger` for customers (App still passed both the full ~12k array),
`AssignAssetPanel` and `ConsumableIssuePanel` for POP / fiber locations. That is
thousands of DOM nodes per form, clipped inside the table's scroll container.

**Fix.** `components/common/SearchPicker.tsx` is now the one interaction core:
minimum query length, debounce, stale-response guard, keyboard navigation,
clear/revert on Escape, a fixed-position portal for scroll containers, and an
explicit failure state with Retry (never "no matches" on a failed read).
`CustomerPicker` and the new `LocationPicker` are thin wrappers, so debounce,
outside-click and failure copy cannot drift per form. Customers stay
server-searched through the paged `/api/customers`; locations filter the
already-loaded bootstrap directory (a small POP/node list, no request per
keystroke), capped at 20 rows matched on name, address or contact.
`CustomersManagement` and `CustomerLedger` no longer take the `customers` array
from App; `LedgerView` keeps its native select for VendorLedger. Issue
Consumables and Assign Fixed Asset pass `portal` so the listbox escapes the
table's horizontal scroller.

**Verified.** `tests/searchPickerPortal.test.ts` (1: renders from body and
selects), `tests/locationPicker.test.ts` (4: type filter, 20-row cap, clear,
failure shape) and `tests/directoryPickers.test.ts` (5 wiring pins: no
`customers.map`, both screens no longer take the array, both wrappers delegate
to the core). Suite **1162 tests, 1160 pass, 2 Windows-only skips, 0 fail**;
`npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Live on the dev app: in Assign Fixed Asset, typing
`Example` into POP / Network Location listed the three POP server rooms with
their address and contact (no full option list), while the committed row stayed
`Example Location 2`; in Issue Consumables, typing `Exam` in the per-line
customer field issued `GET /api/customers?query=Exam&page=1&pageSize=20` → 200
and rendered the four Example customers in a body-level portal, unclipped by
the table; Escape reverted the field to the committed selection. No records
were written.

## ✅ U-1 shipped — one searchable customer picker (2026-10-24)

**Problem.** The customer field already searched, but each copy filtered the
full `customers` bootstrap array: `SalesInvoices` and `ProductSalePanel` held
two near-identical inline pickers (each capped its own list), while
`AssignAssetPanel` still rendered every customer as a native `<option>`. At
~12k customers that is thousands of DOM nodes per form and an unusable
scroll list.

**Fix.** `components/common/CustomerPicker.tsx` is now the one control: 2+
characters, debounced, server-searched through the existing paged
`/api/customers` endpoint, capped at 20 rows, with keyboard navigation, an
explicit failure state + Retry (never “no matches” on a failed read), and a
stale-response guard. The screen supplies the API method
(`search={api.searchCustomers}`), so the per-screen surface pins still see the
read. `AssignAssetPanel`, `SalesInvoices` and `ProductSalePanel` all use it;
the old inline copies and their full-array filters are gone. The deployment
line also keeps the picked directory row, so submit no longer depends on the
customer still being in a stale bootstrap snapshot. No new screen, so the
screen-count tables did not change; the three surface pins gained
`searchCustomers`.

**Verified.** `tests/customerPicker.test.ts` (5 jsdom cases: minimum length +
debounce, click/clear, failed search + Retry, keyboard selection, slow-response
discard). Live on the dev app: typing `Example` in Assign Fixed Asset issued
`GET /api/customers?query=Example&page=1&pageSize=20`, listed the four
directory rows with phone/address, and selected
`Example Customer 1 (CUS-10291)`; the same control rendered and searched in
Product Sale and Create Sales Invoice. Form reset afterwards; no records were
written. Suite **1152 tests, 1150 pass, 2 Windows-only skips, 0 fail**;
`npx tsc --noEmit` clean.

## ✅ DF-13…DF-17 close-out review (2026-10-24)

and guards for claims that no longer describe it. Stale statements in the docs
and guards were corrected, and one residual was closed:

  path is what called `loadSellableSerials`; when the bootstrap itself failed
  after a branch switch, the loader never ran and the previous branch's units
  stayed in the Sell-to-Customer form. `refreshAllData`'s non-401 catch now

  Pinned by two added assertions in `tests/df17SilentReads.test.ts` (count

- **Task list** now records the close-out cross-checks and the residual under

  fetch shim returned 503 for `/api/bootstrap` and `/api/serial-log`, then the

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean; the `df13`, `df14`, `df15`, `df17`,

**1152 tests, 1150 pass, 2 Windows-only skips, 0 fail** (docs-count gate
included).

## ✅ DF-17 shipped — the last three silent reads are visible and retryable (2026-10-24)

**Problem.** Three failures were still indistinguishable from valid data: the
cached-payload settings backfill dropped `/api/settings` errors (the app ran on
`{}` defaults, so the company-wide blind count read false while the database
may hold true); a failed sellable-serial read kept the previous branch's units,
so the Sell-to-Customer form offered serials the server would refuse; and a
rejected permission save whose re-read also failed left the rejected edits
looking trustworthy.

**Fix.** Settings: `loadSettingsFromServer` records `settingsBackfillError` and
App renders a “company settings” `LoadStateNotice` with Retry; the audit
screen's company-wide blind toggle is disabled and reads `UNKNOWN` while the
setting is unknown or still loading. Sellable serials: `loadSellableSerials`
clears the list the moment the branch scope changes, records
`sellableSerialsError`, and `SalesInvoices` renders a retryable notice and
refuses to pin a serial to “not in inventory” when the list is unread (App
passes the state to both render sites). Permissions:
`resyncMatrixFromServer` records `matrixResyncError`, a `saveBlocked` flag
disables Save and Reset, and the notice offers Retry; a successful re-read
applies the server matrix through the existing update event.

**Verified.** `tests/df17SilentReads.test.ts` (6 source pins: the backfill
loader and notice, the toggle's unknown state, branch-scope clearing and the
form's gate/notice, and the permission resync/disabled-Save wiring). Full suite
**1152 tests, 1150 pass, 2 Windows-only skips, 0 fail**; `npx tsc --noEmit`
clean. The manual forced-failure limitation was closed by the close-out
review's run above (bootstrap + serial-log 503s, then a Retry that repopulated
WH001); all three remain UI wiring around existing fetch paths, pinned at
source like the other screen-level fixes (A6).

## ✅ DF-16 shipped — one branch-safe, retryable exchange-device read (2026-10-24)

**Problem.** Every StockOperations host fetched the exchange tab's deployed
customer devices itself (up to 11 mounted hosts), and a failed read only logged
to the console: `exchangeCustomerDevices` was replaced on success, so a failed
branch switch kept the previous branch's units on screen under the new branch's
name, with no notice and no Retry.

**Fix.** `services/exchangeDeviceStore.ts` is a pure per-branch cache (one
request per scope, request tokens, a cleared scope plus error on failure) and
`services/exchangeDeviceCache.ts` binds it to React through
`useSyncExternalStore`. The host uses `useExchangeDevices(selectedBranchId)`
and passes rows/loading/error/retry through the panel context; the panel
renders `LoadStateNotice` with a Retry, no longer claims “no devices match”
while the read failed, and refreshes the shared list after a successful
exchange instead of calling `api.getCustomerDevices` itself. App invalidates
the cache on `CUSTOMER_DEVICES` / `ASSETS` SSE bursts and full refreshes, and
resets it on sign-out.

**Verified.** `tests/exchangeDevices.test.ts` (12 tests): 8 store tests (shared
read per branch, branch-switch isolation, failure clears + error + Retry,
coalesced invalidation, superseded answers, sign-out reset, scope keys) and 4
wiring pins (App invalidation/reset, host cache use with no direct fetch,
panel notice/refresh, one shared store). Updated pins:
`registerRefreshDomains.test.ts` (the host now makes no server call and the
panel no longer fetches devices) and `df12Cleanup.test.ts` (post-exchange
refresh goes through the cache). Full suite **1141 tests, 1139 pass, 2
Windows-only skips, 0 fail**; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. No manual two-host pass
with a forced failure was run; the cache behaviour is covered by the pure store
tests and the wiring by source pins.

## ✅ DF-15 shipped — a failed serial lookup is no longer “not in the register” (2026-10-24)

**Problem.** `DeviceSerialInput` mapped every rejected lookup to `not-found`,
and both exchange dialogs pre-mapped the rejection to `null`
(`.catch(() => null)`). A 500/503/dropped request therefore read exactly like
the register's definitive “no such unit” — with no Retry — inviting a re-scan of
a valid serial or an escalation for the wrong reason.

**Fix.** `DeviceSerialState` gained `lookup-failed`, rendered amber as “Could
not check the register” with a Retry that re-runs `resolve`. The catch now
reads the status `fetchJson` attaches to the rejection: only 404 sets
`not-found`. Both dialogs pass the inline rejection through to the field and
stop the submit path with the shared `SERIAL_LOOKUP_FAILED_MESSAGE` on a
non-404, before any refusal or audit report; a real 404 still goes through
`describeReplacementRefusal`.

**Verified.** `tests/df15SerialLookupFailure.test.ts` (8 tests): pure status
classification (404 vs 500/503/403/transport), a rendered failure state whose
Retry re-issues the lookup, a rendered 404 that still says “Not in the serial
register for this branch” with no Retry, a resolved row still filling PON/MAC
in canonical form, and the two dialogs' wiring. Full suite **1129 tests, 1127
pass, 2 Windows-only skips, 0 fail**; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. The failed-read
path was exercised through the component itself (jsdom), not a live outage.

## ✅ DF-14 shipped — party ledgers follow live ledger events (2026-10-24)

**Problem.** Both ledgers fetched only on party change and tab re-activation,
and App passed no refresh key: recording a payment, reversing one or posting a
return anywhere else updated the bootstrap invoice slices but left the open
ledger at its activation-time closing balance until the operator left and
re-entered the tab.

**Fix.** `VendorLedger` takes the procurement register counter
(`registerRefresh.purchaseInvoices`); `CustomerLedger` takes the sales counter
plus the shared returns counter (a posted or cancelled credit note moves the
receivable too). Both include the counter(s) in their fetch effect, so a burst
re-reads the selected party in place while the tab is open. The read stays a
no-op while no party is selected, and — following DF-13's `useTabActive`
precedent — a hidden keep-mounted tab skips it; the next activation already
re-runs the fetch. The DF-08 sequence guard and the Load button are unchanged.

**Verified.** `tests/df14LedgerRefresh.test.ts` (4 tests) pins the props, the
effect dependencies, both no-request guards (empty picker, hidden tab), App's
counter wiring and the retained sequence guard. The register guard's wiring
check now counts per render site instead of app-wide (a counter can feed two
components since DF-14), and `MOUNT_REFETCH_NO_SSE_KEY` became
`MOUNT_REFETCH_SCREENS` with the two ledgers exempted from “no register
wiring” and pinned positively by the new test. Full suite **1121 tests,
1119 pass, 2 Windows-only skips, 0 fail**; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. No manual
browser pass was run; the ledgers' fetch is an ESM API import with no
behavioural harness (A6), hence the source pin.

## ✅ DF-13 shipped — Financial Statements follows the live summary (2026-10-24)

**Problem.** `FinancialStatements` fetched its per-branch matrix only on tab
activation and `figuresFor` preferred that map over the `financialSummary`
prop. DF-05 keeps the prop fresh on every owning domain, so the activation-time
map shadowed the live value: a sale/payment/purchase posted elsewhere left the
statement matrix unchanged while the closing wizard moved.

**Fix.** The batch effect now re-runs when `financialSummary` changes, and the
memoized statement/comparison tables depend on it too (the fallback path was
keeping a stale closure). A new `useTabActive` hook skips the prop-driven read
while the keep-mounted tab is hidden, so a stock edit elsewhere no longer
issues a branch × FY batch nobody can see; the next activation re-runs it. The
map is deliberately not cleared on a FY switch — its keys include the FY id,
and clearing would make the prior-year column fall back to the current summary
mid-read. Activation and Retry still trigger the batch.

**Verified.** `tests/df13StatementsRefresh.test.ts` pins the visible-only
read, the `financialSummary` dependencies and the remaining triggers; the new
`useTabActive` hook has a render test in `tests/keepMountedRenderCount.test.ts`
and the Retry dep pin in `tests/closeoutResiduals.test.ts` was updated. Full suite
**1117 tests, 1115 pass, 2 Windows-only skips, 0 fail**; `npx tsc --noEmit`
clean. No manual browser pass was run for this task; the screen's fetch is an
ESM API import with no behavioural harness (A6), hence the source pin.

## ✅ Fresh data-fetching pass — all five follow-ups shipped (2026-10-24)

A second read-only pass over the self-fetching screens, the cached-bootstrap

the work items are DF-13…DF-17 in

   Both ledgers take the existing register counters and re-read the selected

4. **P2 — Exchange device list is stale across failed branch switches** and is
